# The case against approving ACD-1.0 — the list, continued

This file continues the list in [`ACD-1.0.against.md`](ACD-1.0.against.md) from #223. The numbering
is continuous, the status notation and the way a row is closed are those stated in that file, and
the totals stated there count the rows of both files. New rows are added at the end of this file.
The list was divided on 2026-11-15 because of its size; no row was renumbered, reworded, or removed.

| # | Adverse fact | Status |
|---|---|---|
| 223 | **The Check that verifies the reviewer's verification command did not run the reviewer's verification command.** Check 472c executes the commands `REVIEWERS.md` gives a reviewer and compares the output against the expected value printed beside them — the layer added after #190 found that page's expected values disagreeing with reality. **Measured 2026-09-24: before running each command it rewrote `shasum -a 256 -c` into `sha256sum -c`.** On Linux the two produce identical output, so CI stayed green and the substitution was invisible. On macOS `sha256sum -c` **does not fall back to standard input when the operand is omitted** — it prints its usage line and verifies nothing — so the freeze check reported *expected 5× OK / got 0× OK* **while the command actually printed on the entry page verified all five digests correctly**. The page was right and the instrument was wrong, and the error was in the direction that costs the most to diagnose: a red gate about the frozen licence text, on the machine where the work is done. **The converse is the part that matters**: had the entry page carried the broken form, this Check would have rewritten it into the working one and reported green — **a gate that substitutes its own input cannot detect a defect in that input** | **[CLOSED]** **The substitution is removed; the command runs verbatim, and the docstring records why.** If `shasum` is absent from an environment the command emits nothing and the Check goes red with the count mismatch, which is the honest outcome — the entry page would then be giving that environment a command it cannot run. **This is #87's lesson on the instrument, applied to an instrument built three days earlier by us**: the first sweep counted licence submissions with a regex that dropped `\bAI\b`, and the correction there was *report the tool beside the number*. Here the tool changed the object before measuring it. **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the other three commands on the page are executed verbatim for the right reasons — they are, and they were never rewritten, but that is because no rewrite happened to be convenient for them, not because a rule forbade it. The rule now exists in the docstring and nowhere else. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 置換をやめ、入口ページの文字列をそのまま実行する。**合否**: **審査者が貼る文字列と、Check が 実行する文字列が同一か。** 片方でも書き換えれば、その Check は入口ページではなく自分の書き換えを検査している。 |
| 224 | **The self-check that verifies the safety net stopped reaching a verdict, and the failure mode is neither red nor green.** `mutation-probe.yml` is the only layer that verifies the Checks actually catch regressions rather than merely existing; it runs weekly and does not gate any pull request. **Measured 2026-09-24 from a dispatched run: the consistency job reached 368 of 372 mutations and hit `timeout-minutes: 30`, ending as `cancelled` with four left.** Cancelled is not failure — **no `All N caught` line is printed, so the run neither confirms the net nor reports a gap**, and nothing goes red for anyone to rerun. **The behaviour job met this exact wall on 2026-08-17 at 55 minutes and was sharded, first four ways and then eight; its sibling was never sharded at all.** The workflow's own comment block records that history in full, three lines above a job that had been left single. **Fixing the layer that hit the wall and leaving the one beside it means meeting the same wall a second time.** The consistency job also never printed an elapsed line, which is the one signal that would have shown the duration growing toward the limit — **the measurement that exists on the sibling and would have made this visible months earlier** | **[CLOSED]** **Sharded four ways, and the elapsed line added.** Coverage was verified rather than assumed: the four shards together cover all 372 mutations exactly once (`index % n`, checked by enumeration). Measured cost before sharding was **4.5s per mutation locally and 4.9s in CI**, from which a shard was estimated at about 7.6 minutes. **That estimate was wrong and the run that followed says so**: the real shards took **426 / 549 / 680 / 714 seconds — a maximum of 11.9 minutes** against the 30-minute limit (2.5× headroom, not 4×), with a **1.68× spread** between shards. Two written premises fell with it: that consistency mutations cost the same each (they do not, though the spread is smaller than the 2× the behaviour side saw), and that per-mutation cost would be unchanged — **the four shards together spent 6.4s per mutation against the single job's 4.9s**, because each shard pays its own checkout and setup. **Sharding buys wall-clock, not total work.** Shard 3/4 was run end to end: **93 mutations, all caught, working tree restored.** The scope line now names the shard (`All 93 mutations in shard 3/4 (of 372 total)`), because a shard that claims the full count lets a reader conclude the net is healthy from a log that verified a quarter of it — **the same false-scope defect that was fixed on the behaviour side in 2026-08-17 and, again, not carried across.** **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the four mutations never reached were passing. They were not run, and the correct statement is that their status is unknown until the next weekly run. **⚠ The converse**: sharding buys headroom, it does not stop the growth. Every mutation added moves the layer back toward the limit, and the elapsed line is now the thing that must actually be read. **潰し方は実行済み** —— consistency 側も 4 分割し、elapsed を必ず出す。**合否**: **週次 run が `cancelled` でなく success か failure で終わっているか。** `cancelled` は「まだ失敗していない」ではなく**結論が無い**状態であり、週次ゆえ誰も rerun しないので、そのまま安全網の検証が止まる。 |
| 225 | **We published a machine-readable index that claimed conformance to a schema which does not exist, and wrote a BLOCKING Check enforcing rules we attributed to a specification nobody here has ever read.** `.well-known/index.json` and `.well-known/agent-skills/index.json` have declared `"$schema": "https://schemas.agentskills.io/discovery/0.2.0/schema.json"` since 2026-08-23, and `.well-known/api-catalog` pointed at the same URL as that resource's `service-desc`. **Measured 2026-09-25, four ways, each with a control**: the host does not exist — `agentskills.io`'s own authoritative nameservers return **NXDOMAIN with the parent SOA** for it while the parent resolves; the Wayback Machine holds **no snapshot of that URL, ever**; the Agent Skills specification site defines **no discovery index, no `.well-known` path and no `$schema`** across all nine pages of its own documentation index; and the official `agentskills/agentskills` repository contains **zero occurrences of `schemas.agentskills.io` and zero of `well-known`** (controls: `SKILL.md` returns 19, a nonsense token returns 0, so the search discriminates). **Check 448 then encoded this**: its docstring stated that *the specification requires name / type / description / url / digest on every entry* and added *"必須 field 名は本 Check にリテラルで持つ (外部仕様なので repo から導出できない)"* — **asserting an external authority for a rule we invented**, and enforcing the presence of the unreachable `$schema` as *"仕様上 required"*. The entries also declared `"type": "skill-md"` while pointing at `llms-full.txt` (plain text) and `AI2AI.md` (no skill frontmatter) — **neither is a SKILL.md in the sense the standard defines**. **This is #77 on the machine-facing surface**: there, the submission requirements were reconstructed from neighbours' experience instead of read at source; here, an entire discovery format was | **[CLOSED]** **The false claims are removed and the Check now says whose rule it is.** `$schema` is gone from both indexes, the dead `service-desc` context is gone from the catalog (8 → 7), and `type` is `document`. The five fields are **kept** — they are good — but the docstring now records that the basis moved from *the specification requires* to *we decided*, and cites the one thing the specification does say that supports them: progressive disclosure, where an agent judges relevance from name and description before fetching the body. **New 448b** forbids a `$schema` outside the origin we serve, with the origin derived from `sitemap.xml` rather than hard-coded; **non-vacuity was proved with the exact declaration this entry is about**. **And the deferred half of #212 now has an answer.** That entry said *"we cannot verify offline whether the discovery schema defines a licence field"* and declined to invent a key — correctly. The specification does define one, and it is not on any index: **`license` is an optional field of `SKILL.md` frontmatter**. The repository's one real skill carries it now. **⚠ What this does not establish**: that the host never existed. NXDOMAIN today from its own authoritative zone, plus no archival trace and no mention in the standard's site or repository, is what was measured; *never* is a stronger word than that evidence. **⚠ The converse, at the same weight.** Publishing our own discovery file under `.well-known/` is not itself wrong — the namespace is extensible, and everything an agent actually uses (name, description, URL, digest) was correct and resolvable the whole time. **Nothing here was unlicensed or unreachable**; what was false was the claim of conformance. And Check 448's *effects* were good: it is why `description` exists on every entry and why the digest prefix is the standard `sha256:`. **The defect was the provenance of the authority, not the content of the rules** — which is the harder kind to see, because everything it produced looked right. **⚠ Removing a false signal is not the same as installing a true one, and the first version of this fix did only the former.** The C6 review named three losses of one shape: an agent could no longer attempt schema validation *at all* (before, it would at least fail loudly); `document` carries less signal than `skill-md` would have carried *had it been real*; and the catalog no longer said this resource has a description. **All three were backfilled rather than accepted**: both indexes now carry a `documentation` field and the catalog's `service-desc` is restored, all three pointing at the mirror document that describes the format — **measured at 200 on the published site**, not assumed. **The trap was obvious once named**: declaring a pointer that nothing verifies is the defect this entry is about, so **448c requires the target to resolve to a file in the repository**, proved RED both by pointing it at a missing file and by dropping the field. **What is still not installed is machine-checkable structure** — the description is prose for a reader, not a schema a validator can run. That is the honest remaining gap. **⚠ The sharpest part is that the discipline meant to prevent this produced it.** On 2026-08-23 the same fetch failed, and the increment note for that day records stopping to ask whether the failure was evidence about the tool or about the world — the right question, recorded as a near-miss avoided. **The conclusion written that day was that Agent Skills is a real standard and *"the URL was as the specification says"*.** The first half was checked. **The second half was never checked at all** — it was inferred from the first. **Suspecting the instrument correctly leaves you with *undetermined*, not with *fine*,** and the note that day converted one into the other. That note now carries a dated correction; the original text is kept. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 存在しない schema への適合主張を消し、Check の典拠を「仕様の要求」から「我々の設計判断」へ書き直し、規範層の check-map が述べていた「**仕様原文で確認**」も訂正した。**合否**: **外部の仕様を典拠として引くとき、その仕様を実際に開いたか。** 開けないなら、その規則は我々のものとして書く。 |
| 226 | **We audited the classification of the entries we had marked as not ours to close, and three of them said, in their own text, that they were not external — and the error ran in the comfortable direction.** Every category this register can close itself had reached zero (`TEXT` / `DOSSIER` / `OPEN` / `UNTRIAGED`), leaving 51 `EXTERNAL` and 28 `TRADE`. **`EXTERNAL` is the most comfortable label available** — it says an item is neither our fault nor our work — so the classification was re-read adversarially against that bias, which is the discipline #140 records and the owner named on 2026-09-13. **Three entries contradicted their own token.** #181's disposition reads *"`EXTERNAL` に分類したのは初回の自動導出の誤りで"* and calls its remedy done. #187's reads *"開示の正確さは我々の側の作業である（外部依存ではない）。⚠ この entry は `EXTERNAL` ではなく実質 `DOSSIER`"*. #192's reads *"注記の有無を機械で見る層が無いことを認めること —— 現在これは習慣であって強制ではない"* — **naming a gate we could build and had not**. **The audit then found the failure #192 describes happening a second time.** `AS-OF.md`'s falsification table states its own rule — *"観測が来たら、まずここを見る"* — and #192 records a row whose condition was satisfied for nine days with nobody going back. **Measured 2026-09-25: #211 had sat in this register since the previous day carrying a senior list participant's statement that existing licences already cover the third gap** (Fontana, *"appropriate even in situations where copyrightability is relatively dubious"*), which runs directly at the row asserting that gap is real. **The row had never heard of it.** **And four of the six rows carried no declared state at all** — not met, not evaluable, nothing. Two of those four turn out to be **unevaluable in principle until we submit**, which is itself a fact the table never said. | **[CLOSED]** **The tokens are corrected, the table now declares a dated state per row, and Check 475 makes the second part structural.** #181 → `CLOSED`, #187 → `DOSSIER` (still open, because the family recurred four times — **but *why it is open* and *who can close it* are different questions, and the token answers the second**), #192 → `CLOSED` by building the layer it named. **Every row of the falsification table now carries 🔴 met / 🟡 not met, with a near miss / ⚪ not met / ⚫ precondition unmet, plus an ISO date**, and the #211 near miss is written in rather than written over, per the table's own rule. **⚠ The scope of Check 475 is stated in its own docstring rather than implied**: it prevents a row going quiet and stale; **it cannot make anyone notice**. Whether a condition has been met is a judgement about the world. **No freshness threshold is set** — a check that reddens on a calendar rots on its own and teaches readers to ignore red. **⚠ The converse, which the audit also produced.** The other 48 `EXTERNAL` entries were read and they are honest: each names what only counsel, OSI, a court, or an adopter can do, and what we can do meanwhile. **The classification was not systematically self-serving — three errors in fifty-one.** **⚠ And two of the three were already confessed in their own prose**, which means the register was more honest than its own machine-readable summary — **the prose knew; the token did not.** That is the reverse of the usual failure here, where the summary is confident and the detail is thin. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 分類を是正し、反証表に状態を宣言させ、Check 475 で構造化した。**合否**: **「我々には閉じられない」と分類したものを、その entry 自身の本文が否定していないか。** `EXTERNAL` は最も居心地のよい分類なので、**その山だけは定期的に、逆向きの圧力をかけて読み直す。** |
| 227 | **The check that stops the submission sending a reviewer to a clause that says nothing was reading four of the thirty-five documents the submission sends them to.** Check 472 exists because clause numbers are not preserved across versions — 1.0's §15.7 and §15.8 are absent from every successor — and #144 records the packet having done exactly that once. **Measured 2026-09-25**: the submission packet and entry page link a reviewer to **35 files**; the check's face list was **four**, written as a literal. **The wider surface is currently clean** — nine reviewer-facing answer documents carry 272 clause citations and **every one resolves in the submission target** — so nothing is wrong today. **What is unguarded is the moment of maximum risk**: when `SUBMISSION-TARGET` moves to a successor, documents like `FAQ.md` become documents about a *different* version, and a literal four-face list sees none of it. **This is the same shape as #194 and as Check 473b's rebuild** — a gate whose stated purpose names a risk, scanning a subset of the surfaces where the risk lives. | **[CLOSED]** **The face set is derived (4 → 25) and the widening was proved on the scenario it exists for.** Two rules only, because complicated scoping is where false positives live: files named `<target>.*` are about that version, plus the version-neutral entry and index pages. **Documents carrying another version's name fall out by construction** — `ACD-1.1-CHANGELIST.md` citing 1.1 clauses is correct, and checking it against the target would be a false positive. **Non-vacuity was measured at the version switch, not with a synthetic number.** A made-up clause number (one belonging to no version) does *not* fire, by design: the check only treats a number as a clause if it is a clause in some version, precisely so dossier section numbers are not misread. So the probe was the real scenario — `SUBMISSION-TARGET` set to 1.2 — and it went RED on `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` and `REVIEWERS.md`. **Stated exactly: the old four-face list would have caught `REVIEWERS.md` on its own; `READY-TO-SUBMIT.md` is the face the widening added.** One face, demonstrated — not twenty-one claimed. **⚠ And widening 472 immediately broke 472b, which was the useful part.** Reusing the derived set for the verbatim-quotation check lit four files and sixteen occurrences, and **reading them showed every one was correct**: `errata.md` quoting the successor's wording is what an errata document *is for*, and `against.md`, `discussion-log.md` and `jurisdictions.md` are records that span versions. **A clause *number* must resolve in the target; a *quotation* may legitimately come from another version in a document whose job is to compare them.** 472b keeps the narrow set, and the measurement is written into the code beside it. **⚠ The converse on the audit that produced this.** The same sweep measured Check 472's coverage at **10.1% of all clause citations in `LICENSES/`** and that number is misleading — the other 89.9% are in documents about other versions, where the narrow scope is correct. **The first framing of a coverage number is usually the wrong one**, and the honest question was not *what fraction does it scan* but *is the set of submission-side faces complete*. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 面の集合を導出型にし、版の切り替えで RED を実測した。**合否**: **走査範囲を literal で書いた Check は、その literal を書いた日の面しか見ない。** 射程が「ある集合のすべて」を名乗るなら、集合は導出する。 |
| 228 | **The base rate that carries this dossier's reading of its own silence had only ever been measured over thirty of the archive's five hundred and six months — and the first attempt to widen it produced a number that was wrong in our favour.** #109 established, over `license-discuss` 2024-01 to 2026-09, that **22% of threads draw no reply at all**, and that figure is load-bearing: it is what licenses our position that the silence following our own post is ordinary rather than diagnostic. **It had never been checked against the rest of the record.** On 2026-09-25 the complete archives of both lists were fetched — **506 months, 199 `license-review` and 307 `license-discuss`, no month missing** — and the rate recomputed. **The first computation said 38.9% on #109's own window, against #109's 22%, and it was wrong.** The detector read only the first physical line of each `Subject:` header; **225 of 491 subject lines in that window are folded across lines**, and the samples show one thread wrapping at *different* points in different messages, so a single conversation was counted as several — most of the fragments single-message, inflating the zero-reply rate. **The error ran toward the conclusion we prefer.** A higher base rate makes our silence more ordinary. | **[CLOSED]** **Fixed, recomputed, and the load-bearing claim survives on an independent instrument.** Unfolding headers and decoding encoded words gives, across the whole record: **`license-review` 591 threads, 138 with no reply — 23.4%**; **`license-discuss` 2,896 threads, 788 with no reply — 27.2%** (digests excluded; the pre-fix figures were 618 / 24.3% and 2,913 / 27.4%). **#109's reading holds at census scale**: silence is an ordinary outcome on both lists, at roughly one thread in four, over twenty-seven years rather than thirty months. **✅ 2026-09-28: the residual is explained, and the explanation ran our way again.** The eight threads are **mailman digests** —— `license-discuss digest, vol N, issue M` takes a different subject every time, so **each one is necessarily counted as a thread with no reply**. Excluding them (`^license-(discuss\|review) digest, vol `) gives **69 threads and 23.2% on #109's own window against #109's 68 and 22% —— the control now reproduces to within one thread**, and the last point of difference is consistent with this cache being twelve days newer. **The corrected whole-record figures are `license-review` 591 threads, 138 with no reply —— 23.4%, and `license-discuss` 2,896 threads, 788 —— 27.2%.** **⚠ The direction is the one this entry warned about**: digests raise the rate, and a higher rate is the reading that suits us. **The fix is in the instrument**, with the measurement that motivated it written beside it. **Neither figure supersedes the other, and both are quoted with the instrument that produced them** (#87). **⚠ What the census does not do.** It measures *threads*, and #139 established that a thread's silence is not the absence of a decision — MIT-I went quiet on the list and was withdrawn four months later in board minutes. **A wider corpus does not widen what the measurement is about.** **⚠ And the control is the whole lesson.** Running the new detector on the old window, purely to see whether it reproduced a known number, is the only reason the fold bug was found; the whole-archive figure looked entirely plausible on its own. **A census is not more trustworthy than a sample — it is bigger. The instrument decides which it is.** **潰し方は実行済み** —— 全 506 か月を取得し、折り返しと encoded-word を正しく扱って測り直した。**合否**: **新しい corpus で数字を出す前に、その道具で既知の数字を再現してみたか。** 再現しないなら、動いたのは世界ではなく道具である。 |
| 229 | **We assumed the unverifiable quotations were a fetching problem, and that fetching the rest of the archive would shrink them. It moved the number by one.** #143's remedy is `verify_dossier_quotations.py` — *"取得すれば再現できる形"* — which carries the implication that the sources are in the archives and simply had not been retrieved. **Measured 2026-09-25 after taking the complete record**: going from 335 months to all 506 moved verified quotations from **357 to 358**. **One hundred and seventy-one additional months of mailing-list archive reached one quotation.** **The remaining gap is not in the archives at all**, and classifying all 72 one by one says why: **41 are our own prose or our own measurements** caught by the detector's emphasis rule (*"the heaviest finding of this session"*, *"the next-unmined list goes stale"*), **4 are the licence's own text**, and the **27 with an external name beside them are largely the off-list correspondence this repository deliberately keeps as stubs** (#217). **So the honest count of quotations attributed to a named outsider whose source we cannot reach is at most 27, not 71 — and #143 stated the larger number.** | **[CLOSED]** **Both halves recorded: the remedy does not work, and the entry overstated the failure against us.** **⚠ The direction matters and this register has a standing problem with it.** #131 and #140 record that a discipline of collecting adverse facts creates pressure to err *adversely*, and that errors in that direction get checked less than errors in our favour. **This is a clean instance**: an entry whose whole subject is *what we cannot verify* counted our own sentences among the things we cannot verify, for nine days, and nobody re-derived it. **⚠ What does not change.** No misquotation has been found in any of the 358 verified, the five highest-stakes quotations still match verbatim, and **27 unreachable external attributions is still 27** — the off-list ones are unreachable *by our own choice*, which is a cost we accepted knowingly and must keep stating (#217), not a cost that has gone away. **⚠ And the census cuts the other way too.** That the full archive adds nothing means the corpus is not the constraint — **so no future increment should propose fetching more of it as a way to close this.** The route, if there is one, is tightening the detector's self-quotation rule, which reduces a number without verifying anything new. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 全 506 か月で測り直し、72 件を 1 件ずつ分類して #143 へ書き足した。**合否**: **「届いていない」と数えたものの中に、自分の言葉が混ざっていないか。** 不利な数を報告する前に、その数を作った道具が何を拾っているかを 1 件ずつ見る。 |
| 230 | **Two independent retrievals of the same thirty-three months agree completely — and getting to that statement took two instrument errors that would each have been a serious false report.** #202 recorded the first corroborated observation in this directory: one month, two lists, byte-identical, supplied by the owner. **On 2026-09-25 the owner supplied 2024-01 to the present** — 64 files across both lists — and the same window was retrieved here independently about six minutes later. **Result: 61 file pairs, 1,109 messages, byte-identical. No difference at all.** And the base rate computed **from the owner's copies alone** — `license-discuss` 2024–2026, 444 messages, 76 threads, **27.6% with no reply** — reproduces exactly what #228 measured on copies we fetched ourselves. **The census now has a second party behind it.** **⚠ The first comparison said three files differed and three were missing.** The owner's filenames carry a `(1)` suffix on whichever of the two lists was downloaded second, and **that order is not constant across months** — six files had it the other way round, so six comparisons were run against the wrong list. Deriving the list from the message tags inside each file instead of from its name brought the count to 61 of 61. **⚠ The second comparison still said one file differed by 211 characters.** It did not. `Path.read_text()` applies universal-newline translation and `bytes.decode()` does not; the file holds exactly 211 carriage returns. **The raw bytes are 162,679 on both sides.** | **[CLOSED]** **Recorded, and the corroboration is the smaller half of what this is worth.** **⚠ What it establishes**: every archive measurement in this dossier over 2024–2026 now rests on two retrievals by two parties rather than one by us, which is the specific weakness #202 named. **⚠ What it does not**: the earlier months — 1999 through 2023, the larger part of the census — remain singly sourced, and nothing here speaks to them. **⚠ And the part worth carrying forward.** Two separate measurement mistakes stood between the raw files and the true answer, and **each would have been a consequential false report**: the first would have said two parties' copies of a public archive disagree; the second would have said an archive file changed between two retrievals six minutes apart — **evidence that the record mutates under us**. Neither was true. **Both were caught by the same move**: when a comparison reports a difference, check that both sides were read the same way before checking what the difference means. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 33 か月分を 2 者で照合し、基準率をオーナーの copy だけで再計算した。**合否**: **比較が差分を報告したとき、差分の意味を調べる前に、両辺が同じ読み方で読まれたかを確かめたか。** 本日この一手が 2 度、重い誤報を止めた。 |
| 231 | **A compression was assessed as having no impact, and the document that depended on the removed words was never opened.** The 1.0 → 1.1 pass compressed §2.5, the estoppel clause. `ACD-1.1-CHANGELIST.md` records it as *"§2.5 圧縮 —— estoppel の芯だけ残した"* and, in its risk table, as impact **無し** — none. **Measured 2026-09-25 by diffing 1.0 against the 1.2 draft sentence by sentence**: the pass removed two things. **(b)** *"That reliance is the purpose of this Dedication and not merely a foreseeable consequence of it"* — the element that separates **intended** reliance from mere foreseeability, and the stronger footing for promissory estoppel in the jurisdictions that draw that line. **(c)** *"and is estopped from doing so to the extent the law of any jurisdiction gives that principle effect"* — the explicit invocation. **And `ACD-1.0.comparison.md` answers a recorded criticism — *"The patent carve-out weakens estoppel defences"* — by quoting both of them verbatim as present features of the instrument.** They are present in 1.0 and absent from every successor. **The answer and the text it rests on had come apart, and the assessment that said so had not looked.** | **[CLOSED]** **Both restored to the 1.2 draft, and the cost is stated where the benefit is.** §2.5 now carries (b) and (c) again; the declared word count moves **5,299 → 5,335, +36 against B3**, which is a real charge against the length objection and not a rounding error. **⚠ Not everything was restored.** 1.0's *"revocable, **unsupported, or otherwise** ineffective"* stays compressed — *"ineffective for want of consideration"* carries it — and `comparison.md`'s quotation of that phrase was aligned to the successor instead. **Restoring words because a document quotes them would let the dossier drive the licence**; the test applied was whether the words do work the remaining text does not. **⚠ What this says about the earlier assessment.** *"影響無し"* was not careless — the covenant does survive, and no recipient gains or loses an obligation. **What it missed is that impact is not only on recipients.** A dossier is part of what a reviewer reads, and a compression that empties one of its answers has an impact the clause-level reading does not see. **The question a compression has to answer is not only *what does the text still do* but *what still quotes the words being removed*.** **⚠ And the converse, which is real.** This was found by diffing whole versions rather than by reading either — the same sweep produced 47 differing sentences, of which **43 were rewording, three were documented drops, and one was this**. **A version diff is cheap and had never been run**; every previous finding of this class (E24, E25, E27, E28) was found by hand, one clause at a time. **潰し方は実行済み** —— (b)(c) を 1.2 へ復元し、費用を changelist と errata E34 に書いた。**合否**: **条を圧縮するとき、その語を引用している文書を検索したか。** 条文の側だけを読んで「影響無し」と書くと、答えの側が空を指していることに気づかない。 |
| 232 | **A pull request was closed as redundant on the strength of a comparison that checked whether the *fix* had landed, and never checked whether the *finding* had.** the pull request 1833 carried the sitemap licence contradiction. It was closed on 2026-09-24 after measuring that main already held the corrected `sitemap.xml` and Check 444h, and that a parallel session's entry #222 already registered the finding — **so the entry it carried was dropped as a duplicate.** **Measured 2026-09-25 at the owner's prompting, by diffing the closed branch against main**: six substantive elements existed only in the closed branch. **The date evidence** (`git log -S` puts the CC BY-NC-ND declaration at 2026-04-25, four months before ACD-1.0 replaced *All Rights Reserved*); **the general form** — withdrawing a restriction means sweeping every surface that *asserts* one, not only those that *name* the licence, and Check 444's six faces were the naming ones; **the §6.5 inversion** — that standard says a permission a machine cannot determine is not a permission, and here **a machine was determining a prohibition we do not assert**; **and the binary observation** — the WebP's XMP was already correct on 2026-08-23, so the sweep of that day **reached metadata embedded inside a binary and missed one plain-text XML element.** **None of the six were in #222.** The closed PR's *code* was redundant; its *reading* was not. | **[CLOSED]** **All six salvaged into #222 as a dated addendum, keeping its history.** **⚠ The closing decision was not careless — it was measured, and it measured the wrong thing.** The comparison asked *is this change already in main*, and answered correctly: the sitemap line was fixed, 444h was implemented, the finding was registered. **What it never asked is whether the entry said anything the registered one did not** — and comparing two registers means reading both, which the file-level diff does not do. **⚠ The general form, which is about parallel work rather than about this PR.** When two sessions find the same defect, their *fixes* converge and their *readings* do not; closing on fix-equivalence discards whichever reading was second. **The cheap guard is the one the owner used**: before discarding, diff the abandoned branch's prose against what landed, not just its code. **⚠ And the converse.** Not closing it was not the alternative — it held a merge commit and could not be rebase-merged, and its `#143` text was the stale version that #229 has since replaced. **Closing was right; closing without extracting was the error**, and the two are separable. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 閉じた branch を main と突き合わせ、6 要素を #222 へ救出した。**合否**: **PR を重複として閉じる前に、その PR の*散文*が、残った側の散文が言っていないことを言っていないか確かめたか。** 修正の等価性は、読みの等価性を意味しない。 |
| 233 | **The thread our gap claim rests on had never been read, and reading it supplies the strongest argument against that claim being a gap at all.** One of the three gaps this instrument stands on is an express patent grant in a public-domain-style tool, and the dossier's warrant for it is that patents were *the* issue on which CC0 stalled at OSI. **Measured 2026-09-25 against the complete archive**: of 51 subject-matter threads with eight or more messages, **16 have never had a single line quoted anywhere in this directory**, and the largest of them is *"CC0 incompliant with OSD on patents"* — **69 messages, `license-review`, March 2012, the very argument the claim cites.** **Reading it confirms the warrant and hands us a counter we had never recorded.** Lawrence Rosen states the problem exactly as we do — *"There are FOSS-friendly software licensors who ... do not include an express patent grant in their software licenses (e.g., BSD). In some cases, they expressly **exclude** a patent grant (e.g., CC0)"* — and Carlo Piana argues the exclusion against the OSD directly. **But Russ Nelson answers with a symmetry we have never mentioned**: *"The BSD has no explicit patent waiver. ... your own logic demands that we deprecate the BSD OR that we give up on requiring patent waivers."* **If an express patent grant cannot be required without unseating BSD, then what we present as filling a gap is closer to a feature the list has declined to require** — and `implicit patent`, `deprecate the BSD` and `no explicit patent` return **zero hits** across `LICENSES/`. **And Nigel Tzeng describes CC0 in 2012 in the words we use for ourselves**: *"CC0 is first a public domain COPYRIGHT dedication with a permissive fallback"* — which is §3 plus §4, named on the list fourteen years before we wrote it. **Added 2026-11-15 (the converse, from a decider)**: Bruce Perens, then OSI's standards chair, `license-discuss` 2018-11-09: *"We assert that the OSD terms apply to patents as well as copyright."* (`reviewer-positions.md` §1.157). It is a statement of position, not a board resolution, and the OSD was not amended; it does not dissolve the Nelson symmetry recorded here, but it shows the decider side has also read the OSD as reaching patents. | **[CLOSED]** **The thread is preserved verbatim in `rounds/` and the counter is recorded rather than answered.** **⚠ This is #92's class at scale.** That entry found analysis in this directory resting on text the repository did not hold, and repaired eight instances. **The sweep that produced this one was mechanical** — every thread on our subject, against every line this directory has ever quoted — and it says the same thing about fifteen more threads. **Eight instances were a list; sixteen threads is a property.** **⚠ What the counter does and does not do.** It does not show the gap is unreal: Piana's objection stands in the same thread, and OSI's disposition of CC0 is what it is. **It shows that the list argued both sides in 2012 and we had only recorded one** — and a reviewer who knows this thread will supply Nelson's point whether or not we do. **Recording it first is the only version of this we control.** **⚠ And the converse about the method.** Two detectors disagreed on which threads were unmined — one scanned whole month files containing the subject line, the other only the messages of the thread — and the looser one reported matches drawn from unrelated threads in the same month. **The stricter count is the one used here.** A census of what we have *not* read is as instrument-dependent as a census of what we have. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 一次資料を `rounds/` へ無改変で保存し、反論を register へ入れた。**合否**: **ある論点を「これが争点だった」と述べるとき、その争点が起きた場所を読んだか。** 要約からの再構成は、反対側の議論を必ず落とす。 |
| 234 | **The check that exists so a local `verify` cannot be green while blind was blind to the directory this dossier lives in.** Check 434 stops `verify` reporting success while untracked files sit in governed directories, because the checks that matter most — mirror bijection, the tracked-file ban, the line ceiling — read `git ls-files` and cannot see what is not staged. **Its scope was `js/`, `e2e/`, `.github/scripts/` and `docs/`. `LICENSES/` was not in it**, and `LICENSES/` is where primary sources are added more often than anywhere else. **Measured the hard way on 2026-09-25**: a 3,712-line archive thread was written into `rounds/`, `npm run verify` returned **exit 0**, and CI then failed on **three checks at once** — 108 (no mirror), 365 (over the 1,000-line ceiling), 454 (over 800 lines and unbudgeted). **All three were invisible locally purely because the file was not yet staged.** | **[CLOSED]** **`LICENSES/` added to the scope, proved RED by putting an untracked file there.** **⚠ The gap is exactly the one the check was written to close, one directory over.** 434 was added because `git ls-files`-based checks are blind to untracked files; **it then enumerated the directories where that had already happened**, which is #194's shape — *a gate whose stated purpose names a risk, scanning a subset of the surfaces where the risk lives*. **⚠ And the incident cost nothing except the cycle, which is the argument for the check.** Nothing shipped; CI caught all three. **What it cost was a red CI on a PR whose diff was fine** — and the failure mode it prevents is the reverse, where the local green is trusted and the directory is one CI does not gate. **⚠ The converse worth stating.** Widening a scope is cheap here only because the four existing entries were already right; **the finding is not that someone chose badly but that the list was written from incidents rather than from the invariant.** The invariant is *every directory whose contents a `git ls-files` check reads*, and that is still an enumeration, not a derivation. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 射程へ `LICENSES/` を足し、未追跡ファイルを置いて RED を実測した。**合否**: **`git add` 前の `verify` は「緑」を意味しない。** 射程が列挙である限り、新しいディレクトリは常にその外側から始まる。 |
| 235 | **The nearest approved precedent for our patent gap was approved for a different reason than ours, and the reason is only in the review thread — not in the licence text we census.** `BSD-2-Clause-Patent` is an OSI-approved permissive licence that adds an express patent grant, which is the shape of the gap Section 8 exists to fill — **one of three** (UPL-1.0 and BlueOak-1.0.0 have the same shape; **corrected 2026-09-25**: this entry first said *"the one"*, which the census it cites contradicts), and **the one whose review thread we have read**. **`ACD-1.0.gap-census.md` already carries it** — the census reads the 141 approved, non-deprecated texts of SPDX 3.29.0 (**corrected 2026-09-25** from *"149"*, which is the 3.28.0 count including deprecated IDs) and correctly reports that the clause exists there. **What a text census cannot report is why the list approved it**, and the 2016 review thread says so in the submitter's own words: *"the desire of certain organizations to have a simple permissive license that is compatible with the GNU General Public License (GPL), version 2, but which also has an express patent grant included."* **That is not our reason.** Ours is a public-domain-style instrument reaching machine learning and text-and-data mining; theirs was GPLv2 compatibility for a permissive base. **The two gaps are adjacent and distinct, and the distinction is only visible in the thread.** **The thread also supplies a submission form we can be measured against** — an approved submission stated a **RATIONALE as a problem with existing licences**, proposed its own **PROLIFERATION CATEGORY** (*"Special Purpose License, or, alternatively, as an approved variant of the BSD 2-clause license"*), and carried a **DISTINGUISHING FROM OTHER OSI-APPROVED LICENSES** section. **Our packet declines to propose a tag** (*"None proposed"*), which is compliant — the process *invites* rather than requires one — **but it is a difference from the approved form, and it is recorded here rather than discovered by a reviewer.** | **[CLOSED]** **The thread is preserved verbatim in `rounds/` (ten parts) and the rationale is recorded where the census cannot reach.** **⚠ The strongest thing in the thread is a failure mode we avoid, and the reason we avoid it is worth naming.** Richard Fontana warned the submitter that reusing Apache-2.0's patent language creates a mismatch — the grant is made by *"each copyright holder and contributor"* while the clause scoping the claims *"assumes that anyone granting the patent license is a copyright holder."* **Section 8.1 cannot have that defect**, because the definition of *Dedicator* — **§1.3 in ACD-1.0, renumbered to §1.4 in the 1.2 draft** — makes it *each* person applying the Dedication and says the instrument *"operates severally as to each"*, so grantor and claim-owner are the same party distributively. **That is structural, not lucky** — but it holds only when the definition and Section 8.1 are read together, which is #157's axis. **⚠ And writing that sentence tripped the class this entry is about.** The first draft cited *Section 1.4* alone; **Check 470 rejected it, because the definition is Section 1.3 in the submission target.** #144 is the entry recording that clause numbers are not preserved across versions, and the sentence that fell to it was in an entry about reading a precedent properly. **A version-scoped number is a claim about which text you are describing**, and the safety net caught it before a reviewer did. **⚠ What this does not do.** It does not make the gap larger or smaller. `gap-census.md` measured the texts and found the clause exists in three permissive licences; **this adds why one of them was approved, which is evidence about the list rather than about the texts.** **⚠ And the converse on method.** The first extraction of this thread pulled **72 messages including four from unrelated threads**, because it matched the licence name in message *bodies*. Matching on the unfolded `Subject:` gives **68 from a single month and a single list**. **A thread is defined by its subject header, not by where its name appears** — and the looser extraction would have put four foreign messages into `rounds/`, which is the one directory whose entire value is that its contents are what they say they are. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 一次資料を `rounds/` へ 10 部で保存し、承認理由と提出の形を register へ入れた。**合否**: **承認済みの隣人について述べるとき、その条文だけを読んだのか、承認された理由まで読んだのか。** テキストの census は前者しか答えない。 |
| 236 | **Four people have said on these lists that an express patent grant is not required, one of them that silence already carries an implicit grant by estoppel, and this dossier had recorded none of them.** The remaining eleven unmined subject threads were read in full at the owner's request — 171 messages. **Four turned out not to be on our subject at all** (a filter matching *model*, *patent* and *public domain* pulled in a thread about source-code obfuscation, one about a Caps Lock patent, one about IETF's RAND policy, and one about liability insurance): **the census of what we have not read over-selected by 36%.** The other seven carried the following. **(a) The patent gap is weighed against us by four independent voices.** Matthew Garrett, 2005: *"The OSD doesn't require that a license give you a patent grant in the first place."* John Cowan, 2014: *"nobody can say that licenses that don't grant patent rights explicitly are not open-source licenses."* Russ Nelson, 2012 (#233). **And Bruce Perens, 2005, goes further**: *"There is an implicit patent grant in the BSD license. If you distribute software embodying your own patents with a license to make use of the software, you are effectively granting an estoppel regarding those patents."* **If that is right, the silence our Section 8 exists to cure may not be silence.** **(b) The estoppel our Section 2.5 builds was attacked on this list in 2004, on the element we restored today.** Daniel Wallace set out promissory estoppel's four elements; **John Cowan objected on element two** — *"When I license my work, I don't **know** that you rely on my promises, since I don't know you at all"* — and **Lawrence Rosen rejected the frame outright**: *"Promissory estoppel is a contract doctrine that has nothing to do with a bare license like the GPL."* **The sentence restored to Section 2.5 hours earlier (E34) is the answer to Cowan's objection** — reliance as *purpose* rather than foreseeable consequence is exactly element two — **and we restored it without knowing the objection existed.** Rosen's is not answered. **(c) The head of OSI said in terms that CC0 was not refused.** Simon Phipps, 2014: *"We did not decide against CC0. ... Creative Commons withdrew it from the approval process, but that's what happened, **not an OSI denial**."* `review-corpus.md` already carried that correction from the thread's subject line; **`comparison.md` §1.4 still heads the section *"the only precedent of our type that stalled at OSI"***. (**Resolved the same day, 2026-09-25 19:32 JST, commit `138f3885`**: the heading now reads *「否決されずに取り下げられた先例」* — withdrawn, not denied. (2026-11-12: changed again, to *「否定的な反応のあとで取り下げられた先例（2012・正式の否決は無い）」*, so that the heading carries both facts; see the note under (c) below.) **The sentence above is kept as written; its present tense was true when written and is not now.**) **And Richard Fontana, in the same thread**: *"I believe CC0 is obviously lowercase-o, lowercase-s open source despite the clause about patents."* **2026-11-12 の追記（(c) の逆側）**: 同じ Richard Fontana 氏は 2019-08-22 に *"for the most part the OSI has not formally rejected licenses at all, yet often the non-approval disposition of a license submission is (at least arguably) equivalent to a rejection, and is often spoken of as a rejection."* と書き、CC0 を *"the "rejection" (i.e., withdrawal after negative license-review reaction) of CC0"* と呼んだ（`rounds/2017-04-and-2019-08-license-lists-bsd-patent-approval-and-cc0-disposition-observed.txt`）。Phipps 氏 2014 の「OSI の拒否ではない」は手続きの上で正しいが、審査の側にいた人が 5 年後に取り下げを却下と同じ側に置いた記録が在る。CC0 を「否決されずに取り下げられた先例」と書くときは、この読みも同じ場所に置く（`comparison.md` §1.4 に足した）。同じ通で同氏は、Adaptive Public License の承認がのちの CC0 の扱いと食い違いうるとも書いた。 **(d) Section 3's central question was argued in 2004 and answered against it.** Arnoud Engelfriet: moral rights *"cannot be transferred or given up"* in most countries and permit the author to act *"also when saying 'this work is in the public domain'"* — and therefore *"it may be impossible for a work to truly enter the public domain in any other way"* than expiry. **That is Section 12's problem stated twenty-two years before we wrote Section 12.** **(e) And one thing runs our way, as an arc rather than a quote.** Intel's 2001 BSD+Patent tied its patent grant to GPL-licensed operating systems and was argued down on OSD 3, 6 and 8 — Cowan: *"this license is in effect specific to Linux"* — **while the 2016 version, whose grant is unconditional, was approved** (#235). **ACD Section 8.2 makes its grants *"subject to no condition"* and not terminable on any ground, with the absence of retaliation stated as deliberate.** | **[CLOSED]** **All seven relevant threads preserved verbatim in `rounds/` (eleven files), and the counters are recorded rather than answered.** **⚠ (a) is the heaviest and it is not new in kind — it is new in weight.** #233 recorded one voice; this makes four, spanning 2005 to 2014, none of them ours to rebut. **The honest position is not that the gap is unreal but that the list has repeatedly declined to treat the absence of a patent grant as disqualifying**, and a reviewer who knows these threads will say so. | 237 | **理事会が実際に不承認を議決した、我々の時代にいちばん近い審査の理由は OSD 違反ではなく「主題」だった。そしてその理由が名指しした語が、ACD-1.0 §1.2 の列挙に在る。** Mulan Open Works Licenses 4 本は 2023-02-20 に `license-review` へ提出され、**2023-09-15 に理事会が不承認を議決した**。委員会の勧告文（Chestek 氏が 2023-09-13 にリストへ投稿）はこう述べる —— *"Resolved that it is the opinion of the OSI that the licenses, **as open culture licenses, are not appropriate for OSI approval**."* 理由は本文の欠陥ではなく、**ライセンスが対象と定義した material の列挙**である —— *"The subject matter of licenses is 'intellectual achievement protected by copyright law ... including but not limited to a written work, a musical work, a fine art work, an architecture work, a photographic work, **an audiovisual work**, a graphic work, and a model work.' Because of the differences in the purposes and goals of these different (albeit related) areas, **open culture licenses are outside the purview of the Open Source Initiative**."* **同じスレッドで委員長は境界も述べている** —— *"the OSI is not in the practice of approving licenses that are not software-specific. ... Sometimes an OSI license will **cross over into data or hardware when tied to software**, but not a license altogether unrelated to software."*（Chestek 氏 2023-08-10）。Piana 氏は *"In general, **all the licenses should be rejected for not being (Open Source) software licenses**."*（2023-07-05）、Berkus 氏は *"OSI has **not previously approved content licenses** ... it would be an **organizational policy change** and therefore not a routine license approval."*（2023-03-01）。**ACD-1.0 §1.2 はこう定義する** —— *"'Work' means the material to which the Dedicator has applied this Dedication, in whole or in part, in any medium and in any form, including source code, object code, documentation, data, metadata, **audiovisual material**, and any collection or compilation of these."* **不承認理由が名指しした語の 1 つが、我々の定義にそのまま在る。** | **[CLOSED]** **記録し、提出パケットで先に自分から名指しした**（`submission.md` §B.0）。**⚠ 差は実在し、それを過小に言わない。** MulanOWL の定義には**ソフトウェアが 1 語も無い**のに対し、§1.2 は **source code / object code から始まり**、PREAMBLE の第 1 文は *"Software and the works that surround it"* である。**Chestek 氏自身の carve-out —— *"cross over into data or hardware **when tied to software**"* —— は ACD の形をそのまま述べている。** だが**それは我々の読みであって委員会の判断ではない**。危険は構造ではなく**読まれ方**にある: §1.2 を単独で読む審査者にとって、この列挙は open culture ライセンスの定義と見分けがつかない。**そして本文は凍結中なので、この増分で打てる手は「先に言う」ことだけだった。** **⚠ 逆側も同じ重さで**: 列挙を狭める案（"audiovisual material" を落とす）は **gap 主張を自分で折る** —— 本 instrument が名乗る用途は「学習されるための著作物」で、データセットと学習済みモデルはまさに非コード資産である。**主題の広さは副作用ではなく設計であり、それを隠すのは候補の改善ではなく開示の後退である。** **合否**: **不承認の理由として実際に使われた語が我々の定義に在ることを、審査者より先に我々が書いているか。** **⚠ 2026-11-04 訂正**: 「列挙を狭めると gap 主張を自分で折る」は誤りだった。§1.3 の列挙は例示（§15.5）なので、語を落としても範囲は変わらない（`ACD-1.0.gap-measurements.md` §1.134）。範囲が縮むのはソフトウェアに限る案だけで、そのときも §8.4 + §8.2 の組み合わせはソフトウェアの Work について残る。語を落とす案を採らない理由は、疑義を見えにくくするだけだからである。 **2026-11-07**: 委員会は 2021 年の CERN-OHL の理由書で審査対象の基準を述べている（*"written such that they can be used for software"*）。ACD はこれに本文で答えるが、§1.2 は CERN より広く、判断は OSI に残る（`board-decisions.md` §2e）|
| 238 | **特許について、リストの扱いは「3 段」である。沈黙は許され、明示的な除外は致命的で、明示的な許諾が実務の標準とされている —— 我々は 1 段目しか記録していなかった（#236 の読み違えの危険）。** **(1) 沈黙は許される。** #236 が 4 人、本増分でさらに 1 人 —— 提出者側弁護士 Karin Rivard 氏（MIT）*"The requirements 氏の反論は「不適合だ」ではなく「賢明でない」だった）。**(2) 明示的な除外は致命的である。** 委員長 Pamela Chestek 氏 2023-08-10 —— *"Two of the licenses ... **are not likely to be approved because they expressly state that they do not grant a AAL does not allow logos, and it was approved before OSD 10"* と、前提の事実の誤りで退けた（`rounds/2007-03-license-discuss-stpl-presumption-from-approved-observed.txt`）。**逆側**: 4 人とも個人の文である。ACD が引く先例の用途（審査の対象になりうる形の記録）は、似た条が在るから通る、という Moen 氏が退けた形とは違う。同じ会話で Lawrence Rosen 氏は引用の義務を *"contrary to open source principles"* とし、依頼の形の通知なら置けると答えた。ACD は帰属も引用も求めない（§10.1） **2026-11-23 の追記（委員長の文・2008・`review-readings-6.md` §1.460・portfolio-d7）**: Russ Nelson 氏 2008-05-26（Iggy Wanna Licence）*"Precedent is a bad word to use. | 241 | **A census of every approved licence found four precedents that narrow our gap claims, and none of them had been discussed anywhere in this dossier.** Measured 2026-09-25 against all 141 OSI-approved, non-deprecated texts of SPDX 3.29.0 (`ACD-1.0.gap-census.md`, generated by `.github/scripts/generate_gap_census.py`). **(1) CERN-OHL-P/S/W-2.0** grant patents over *"Products"*, defined as *"any device, component, work or physical object … arising from the use, application or processing of Covered Source"* — **a reading on which a model trained on Covered Source is a Product is open**, so *"no approved licence grants patents that reach a trained model"* cannot be asserted; what holds is that none **names** models, parameters or outputs. **(2) UPL-1.0, BlueOak-1.0.0 and BSD-2-Clause-Patent** already combine a permissive grant with an express patent grant under one notice condition; BlueOak's grant is unlimited by activity (*"everything with this software"*), so training is covered in terms. **(3) EUPL-1.1/1.2** already contain the structure of Section 12: *"In the countries where moral rights apply, the Licensor waives his right to exercise his moral right to the extent allowed by law"*. **(4) WordNet** grants *"Permission to use, copy, modify and distribute this software and database"* — a database as licensed material, though no right is named. | **[CLOSED]** **Recorded where a reviewer meets it**: `submission-reference.md` §2 now carries CERN-OHL-P-2.0, the UPL / BlueOak / BSD-2-Clause-Patent table with what it concedes, WordNet, and EUPL on the moral-rights row; `REVIEWERS.md` narrows the patent limb to *"names models"*; `comparison.md`'s table no longer says the Unlicense and CC0 are silent on successors (both say their dedication binds *"heirs and successors"*). **What survives on every row is the same thing: the condition.** Each precedent keeps at least one; ACD-1.0 keeps none (4.3, 10.1). **A reviewer who thinks a single notice condition is not worth a new licence has answered against us, and that answer is fair** (#84). **⚠ What this does not do**: it does not measure effect. Absence of a word is not absence of a right, and presence of a broad word (*work*, *everything*) is not a holding that it reaches a model. **潰し方は実行済み** —— 全数で当て、最も近い反例を提出参考資料に先に置き、gap の主張を実測で立つ範囲へ狭めた。**合否**: **既存ライセンスが X を持たないと書くとき、全数で当てたか、選んだ比較対象で当てたか。** |

rights"* と述べており、**現役の委員が、我々の特許除外の論点の先例として名指ししている 3 件のうち 2 件を、我々は読んでいない。** **潰し方**: 通数順ではなく**「委員が名指ししたもの」→「我々の類型に形が近いもの」→「通数」**の順に読む。**合否**: **未読の在庫を、通数ではなく我々の論点への近さで並べているか。** **2026-11-16 閉じた（portfolio-30）**: 残っていた 3 段目（C-FSL・CAL 本体・S-FSL・netX・TVA・MOSL）は、2026-11-15〜16 に 5 人で未読の会話を 0 にした作業の中で、どれも `READ-THREADS.txt` に読了の行を持つ（C-FSL は `review-readings-2.md` §1.230、netX は §1.236）。**逆側**: 未読 0 は census の候補（決める側が発言した会話）で数えたもので、この行が数えた「提出名」の 146 件で数え直してはいない。提出名で未言及のものが残るかは、名の一覧を作り直して当てるまで分からない。 |

It applies equally to all of those"* である。**ドシエは人格権を「gap か mission creep か」の分岐として記録してきた**（bottleneck B8・`against.md` #55 系）。**OSD の起草者の読みでは、人格権は OSD が既に射程に入れている制限の 1 つであり、分岐の片側（mission creep）は成り立ちにくくなる。** | **[CLOSED]** **記録した。** **有利**: §12 は人格権を放棄または不行使とするので、**OSD が制限とみなす手段を 1 つ潰している。** §1.59 の「日本では著作権のみを述べるライセンスは改変を保証できない」（Open Source Group Japan 会長の 2024 年の主張）と合わせると、**§12 は「余計なことをしている」のではなく「OSD が要求する自由を droit d'auteur 法域で実際に届かせるための条」と読める。** **⚠ 逆側は 2 つある。** **(1)** これはリスト上の 1 発言であって、OSI の判断でも規則でもない。**起草者の読みは権威だが、承認は委員会と理事会が行う**（`board-decisions.md` の *"prior approval does not bind"*）。**(2) そして有利に読みすぎると B8 のもう半分が消える** —— §12 への実際の批判は「OSD 違反だ」ではなく **「人格権は licensable でない」**（Piana 氏・#104）と **「1 つの instrument に 1 つの権利」**（Landley 氏・#102）で、**本発言はそのどちらにも答えていない。** **合否**: **「OSD が人格権を見ている」と「§12 が有効である」を混ぜていないか。** |

として、*"It has not been submitted for certification in proper form."*、*"The Board considers it a vanity license."*、*"The Board believes that it substantially duplicates an existing license."* の 3 つを挙げた（`rounds/2012-06-license-discuss-open-source-but-not-osi-approved-observed.txt`）。2013 年の同氏の「無制限の裁量」（#323）と同じ線で、ACD に当たるのは 2 つ目（vanity・#255）と 3 つ目（重複・B10）。**逆側**: 2025 年の理事会は OSD 違反だけを却下の理由にすると述べている（`board-decisions.md` §2） **2026-11-12 の追記（曖昧さの逆側・2007）**: Moen 氏の *"unjustifiably opaque or ambiguous in its wording"* には、それが承認を止めなかった記録がある。Microsoft Community License（2007-08）で Lawrence Rosen 氏は file 単位の条件が .zip 全体に及ぶかを問い、*"Not that this would affect its compliance with OSD at all, it just possibly turns a reasonable reciprocity requirement into a very dangerous one."* と書いた。提出者は曖昧さを認めたが、10 月に名だけ変えて出し直した Microsoft Reciprocal License の本文は 1 語も変わらず、OSI の一覧の API は承認日を 20071010 とする（`review-readings.md` §1.193）。**逆側**: 提出者は Microsoft で、曖昧さは 1 つの条の 1 つの場面だった。審査者が本文を変えないことを許したのか、問題にしなかったのかは記録から決まらない。ACD の語の質が問われないことは示さない **同じ追記の 2 つ目（「OSD だけを見よ」の前提・2007）**: GPLv3 の審査の進め方の会話（`license-discuss` 2007-07〜08・84 通・`rounds/2007-07-license-discuss-gplv3-review-scope-observed.txt` に 2 通）で、Luis Villa 氏は審査を OSD への適合だけに絞るよう求め、その理由を GPLv3 の側の事実に置いた —— *"OSI typically wants to know if the existence of a new license is justified; I'd suggest that the extensive review process and very quick adoption of the license indicates very strongly that this is the case."*、*"the license has already received the most extensive public review of any license in history, and is finalized, so discussion of potential flaws in the license (except inasmuch as they may impact OSD-compatibility) are not useful."* Lawrence Rosen 氏も *"GPLv3 is obviously OSD-compliant. Let's get right to the point."* と書いた。**ACD への当たり方**: 審査を OSD だけに絞る主張は、正当性（採用）と、本文の欠陥の議論が済んでいること（広い公開の検討）を前提にしていた。ACD はどちらも持たない（B2・B1）ので、この会話は「ACD も OSD だけで見てよい」という根拠にならない。むしろ、その 2 つが無い提出では OSD の外の議論が残ることの記録である。**逆側**: 2025 年の理事会の文（OSD 違反だけを却下の理由にする・上の追記）は前提を付けていない。Villa 氏の文は 2007 年の一参加者の提案である |
